4174               	;
4175               	; 	' HARRY'S SPEED IS IN QUARTER PIXELS, AND IT DOES NOT RAMP. It used to be
4176               	; 	' 2 px/frame against Kelly's 3, which sounds like a comfortable 1.5x --
4177               	; 	' and Kelly still lost the race to the roof by ELEVEN SECONDS, every
4178               	; 	' round, because the two routes are not the same length. Kelly runs
4179               	; 	' 7,992 px (three traverses plus the roof); Harry runs 4,104, because he
4180               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase
4181               	; 	' resolves on PATH / SPEED, and Kelly needs to be more than TWICE
4182               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.
4183               	; 	'
4184               	; 	' 1.75, AND THE NUMBER IS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Tracked off the
4185               	; 	' reference video (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen
4186               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4187               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.
4188               	; 	'
4189               	; 	' 2.0 would match the original exactly and still cannot be used, because
4190               	; 	' OUR routes are not the original's: Kelly's is 1.95x Harry's here, so a
4191               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves him 5.2 s ahead at the escape edge -- less
4192               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a
4193               	; 	' hit. That figure counts the LIFT, which is on his way and carries him
4194               	; 	' two floors; the foot-only route is 5 s worse and is not the route
4195               	; 	' anybody takes.
4196               	; 	'
4197               	; 	' THE PER-KROOK DIAL IS STILL NOT HIS SPEED. It is the obstacles, which
4198               	; 	' is also the only dial the manual names (`obsp` above, and the ball
4199               	; 	' arcs). assets/checkchase.py checks all of this mechanically -- run it
4200               	; 	' if you touch these numbers.
4201               	; 	hsp4 = 7			' 1.75 px/frame, every Krook

4226 B8CE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4227 B8D0 0450  20 	b *r0
4228               	;
4229               	; 	' ======================================================================
4230               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4231               	; 	' ======================================================================
4232               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4233               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4234               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4235               	; 	' of running.
4236               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4237               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4238               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4239               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4240               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4241               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4242               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4243               	; 	'
4244               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4245               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4246               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4247               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4248               	; 	'
4249               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4250               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4251               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4252               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4253               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4254               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4255               	; 	'
4256               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4257               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4258               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4259               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4260               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4261               	; 	'
4262               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4263               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4264               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4265               	; draw_screen:
4266               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4267               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4268 B8D2 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8D4 A980     
4269 B8D6 E7C2     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY
4270               	; 	GOSUB load_band
4271 B8D8 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8DA A980     
4272 B8DC BCC6     	data cvb_LOAD_BAND
4273               	; 	FOR dq = 0 TO 3
4274 B8DE 04C0  14 	clr r0
4275 B8E0 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DQ
     B8E2 2192     
4276               cv147
4277               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4278               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4279               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4280               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4281               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4282               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4283               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4284               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4285               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4286               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.
4287               	; 		WAIT
4288 B8E4 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8E6 A980     
4289 B8E8 A656     	data wait
4290               	; 		dlv = 3 - dq
4291 B8EA D020  34 	movb @cvb_DQ,r0
     B8EC 2192     
4292 B8EE 0980  32 	srl r0,8
4293 B8F0 C040  18 	mov r0,r1
4294 B8F2 0200  20 	li r0,3
     B8F4 0003     
4295 B8F6 6001  18 	s r1,r0
4296 B8F8 0A80  32 	sla r0,8
4297 B8FA D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DLV
     B8FC 20E1

4373 B9BC C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4374 B9BE 0450  20 	b *r0
4375               	;
4376               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4377               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4378               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4379               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4380               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4381               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4382               	; 	'
4383               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4384               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4385               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4386               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4387               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4388               	; 	'
4389               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4390               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4391               	; 	' above it at all.
4392               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4393               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4394               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4395               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4396               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4397               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4398               	; 	'
4399               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4400               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4401               	; beam_one:
4402               cvb_BEAM_ONE
4403               	; 	IF bt > 2 THEN RETURN		' the roof has no band above it

4938               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.

4940               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
4941               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
4942               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
4943               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
4944               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
4945               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
4946               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
4947               	; 			'
4948               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
4949               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
4950               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.

4953               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs

5116 BF60 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5117               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5118               	; 			'
5119               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5120               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5121               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5122               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5123               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5124               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5125               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5126               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5127               	; 			'
5128               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5129               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5130               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5131               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5132               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5133               	; 			'
5134               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5135               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5136               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5137               	; 			'
5138               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5139               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5140               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5141               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5142               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5143               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5144               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5145 BF62 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BF64 21CA     
5146 BF66 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     BF68 1F00

     C5F6 C694     
5921               cv292
5922               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
5923               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
5924               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
5925               	; 		'
5926               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
5927               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
5928               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
5929               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
5930               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
5931               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
5932               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
5933               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
5934               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
5935               	; 		'
5936               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
5937               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
5938               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
5939               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
5940               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
5941               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
5942 C5F8 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0

6571               	;
6572               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6573               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6574               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6575               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6576               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6577               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6578               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6579               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6580               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6581               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6582               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6583               	; 	'
6584               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6585               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6586               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6587               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6588               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.
6589               	; try_esc:

7147               	;
7148               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7149               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7150               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7151               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7152               	; 	'
7153               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7154               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7155               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7156               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7157               	; 	'
7158               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7159               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7160               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7161               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7162               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7163               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7164               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7165               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7166               	; 	'
7167               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7168               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7169               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7170               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7171               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7172               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7173               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7174               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7175               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7176               	; 	'
7177               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7178               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7179               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7180               	; wipe_shelf:
7181               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7182               	; 	wsn = 8

7253               	;
7254               	; 	' A PILLAR'S CAP MAY BE CLEARED; AN END WALL'S MAY NOT. beam_one stamps
7255               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
7256               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7257               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7258               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7259               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
7260               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the
7261               	; 	' BUILDING'S OUTSIDE WALL, carrying its brick bond up through the floor
7262               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7263               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7264               	; 	' nothing.
7265               	; 	'
7266               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
7267               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight
7268               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize
7269               	; 	' is pushed right out against the end wall; on every other screen no
7270               	; 	' fixture sits close enough for the two-cell clear at #bca and #bca+1 to
7271               	; 	' reach column 0 or 31.

7326               	; 	' THE SAME DISTINCTION ONE ROW LOWER. A radio or a prize stands in the
7327               	; 	' bottom half of a band, so the two cells above it have to be cleared or
7328               	; 	' the pillar it covers keeps its top half hanging in the air. That clear
7329               	; 	' used to be an unconditional green VPOKE, which is only correct when the
7330               	; 	' cell really is a pillar: at column 0 and 31 it is the OUTSIDE WALL, and
7331               	; 	' on the roof it is the SKYLINE. Painting green over either punched a
7332               	; 	' two-row hole through the building -- and because the band is blitted and
7333               	; 	' then corrected within the same pass, you could watch it happen at round
7334               	; 	' start: the wall went up and was taken straight back down again.
7335               	; 	'
7336               	; 	' So it tests for the thing it is allowed to remove rather than trusting
7337               	; 	' the position. COUNTR is a pillar or a counter; anything else stays.

8253               	;
8254               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
8255               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
8256               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
8257               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
8258               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
8259               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
8260               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
8261               	; 	'
8262               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8263               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8264               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8265               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8266               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8267               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8268               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8269               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8270               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8271               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8272               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8273               	; 	' flight looking busy.
8274               	; 	'
8275               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
8276               	; 	'
8277               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
8278               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
8279               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
8280               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
8281               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
8282               	; 	'
8283               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,

8818 DB6E 0450  20 	b *r0
8819               	;
8820               	; 	' ======================================================================
8821               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
8822               	; 	' ======================================================================
8823               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
8824               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
8825               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
8826               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
8827               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
8828               	; 	'
8829               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
8830               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
8831               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
8832               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
8833               	; 	' broken.
8834               	; 	'
8835               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
8836               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
8837               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
8838               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
8839               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
8840               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
8841               	; 	'
8842               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
8843               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
8844               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
8845               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
8846               	; coll_obst:
8847               cvb_COLL_OBST
8848               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
8849 DB70 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DB72 218E     
8850 DB74 0201  20 	li r1,768   ; 3*256
     DB76 0300     
8851 DB78 9001  18 	cb r1,r0
8852 DB7A 1302  14 	jeq cv650
8853 DB7C 0460  28 	b @cv649
     DB7E DB84     
8854               cv650
8855 DB80 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0

9383               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
9384               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
9385               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
9386               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
9387               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
9388               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
9389               	; 	'
9390               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
9391               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
9392               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
9393               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.

9428               	; 	' KELLY FLASHES WHILE HE IS KNOCKED ABOUT. Hitting an obstacle set a
9429               	; 	' 20-frame `knock` and took nine seconds off the clock, and NOTHING on
9430               	; 	' screen said so -- the time simply went. A flash is the arcade's own
9431               	; 	' idiom for "that hit you", costs one variable, and is readable even
9432               	; 	' when the collision happened off the edge of the player's attention.

9456               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
9457               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
9458               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
9459               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
9460               	; 	'
9461               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
9462               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
9463               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
9464               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
9465               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
9466               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
9467               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
9468               	; 	ky = flry(klv)

9593               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.

10388 E846 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10389 E848 0450  20 	b *r0
10390               	;
10391               	; 	' ======================================================================
10392               	; 	' THE SCANNER
10393               	; 	' ======================================================================
10394               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
10395               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
10396               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
10397               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
10398               	; 	'
10399               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
10400               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
10401               	; 	'
10402               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
10403               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
10404               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
10405               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
10406               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
10407               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
10408               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
10409               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
10410               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
10411               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
10412               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
10413               	; esc_tick:
10414               cvb_ESC_TICK
10415               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
10416               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
10417               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
10418               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10419               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
10420 E84A D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E84C 217C     
10421 E84E 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E850 0100     
10422 E852 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10423 E854 1402  14 	jhe cv782
10424 E856 0460  28 	b @cv781
     E858 E86E     
10425               cv782
10426               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
10427 E85A D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E85C 217C     
10428 E85E 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     E860 0700     
10429 E862 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10430 E864 1A02  14 	jl cv784
10431 E866 0460  28 	b @cv783
     E868 E86E     
10432               cv784
10433 E86A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10434 E86C 0450  20 	b *r0
10435               cv783
10436               	; 	END IF
10437               cv781
10438               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
10439               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
10440               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
10441               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
10442               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
10443               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
10444               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
10445               	; 	'
10446               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
10447               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
10448               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
10449               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
10450               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
10451               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
10452               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
10453               	; 	escp = escp + 1
10454 E86E D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     E870 20B0     
10455 E872 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     E874 0100     
10456 E876 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     E878 20B0     
10457               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
10458 E87A 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     E87C 0400     
10459 E87E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10460 E880 1402  14 	jhe cv786
10461 E882 0460  28 	b @cv785
     E884 E88C     
10462               cv786
10463 E886 04C0  14 	clr r0

     E88A 20B0     
10465               cv785
10466               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
10467               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
10468               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
10469               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
10470               	; 	' 7 an east one.
10471               	; 	'
10472               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
10473               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
10474               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
10475               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
10476               	; 	'
10477               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
10478               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
10479               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
10480               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
10481               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
10482               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
10483               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
10484               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
10485               	; 	'
10486               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
10487               	; 	' move at all.
10488               	; 	IF klsc = 0 THEN

10747 EAC8 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10748 EACA 0450  20 	b *r0
10749               	;
10750               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
10751               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
10752               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
10753               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
10754               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
10755               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
10756               	; 	'
10757               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
10758               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
10759               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
10760               	; 	'
10761               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
10762               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
10763               	; 	'   +2  GREY
10764               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
10765               	; 	'
10766               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
10767               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
10768               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
10769               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
10770               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
10771               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
10772               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
10773               	; 	'
10774               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
10775               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
10776               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
10777               	; 	'
10778               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
10779               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
10780               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
10781               	; scan_furn:
10782               cvb_SCAN_FURN

11062               	; 	' moves aside to make room -- the pixels are simply ORed together and the

11200               	;
11201               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
11202               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
11203               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
11204               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
11205               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
11206               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11207               	; scan_wipe:
11208               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
11209               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
11210               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
11211               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
11212               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
11213               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
11214               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
11215               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
11216               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
11217               	; 	'
11218               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
11219               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
11220               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
11221               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
11222               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
11223               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
11224               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
11225               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
11226               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
11227               	; 	'
11228               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
11229               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
11230               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
11231               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
11232               	; 	'
11233               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
11234               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
11235               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
11236 EE22 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     EE24 1680     
11237 EE26 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__SWA
     EE28 20D6     
11238               	; 	FOR swj = 0 TO 5
11239 EE2A 04C0  14 	clr r0
11240 EE2C D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SWJ
     EE2E 2091     
11241               cv840
11242               	; 		FOR swi = 0 TO 63
11243 EE30 04C0  14 	clr r0
11244 EE32 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SWI
     EE34 208F     
11245               cv841
11246               	; 			VPOKE #swa,0
11247 EE36 04C0  14 	clr r0
11248 EE38 064A  14 	dect r10

     EEB4 21D3     
11306               	;
11307               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
11308               	; 	'
11309               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
11310               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
11311               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
11312               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
11313               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
11314               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
11315               	; 	'
11316               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
11317               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
11318               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
11319               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
11320               	; 	'
11321               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
11322               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
11323               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
11324               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
11325               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
11326               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
11327               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
11328               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
11329               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
11330               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
11331               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
11332               	; 	say = 3 - elvl
11333 EEB6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ELVL,r0
     EEB8 2133     
11334 EEBA 0980  32 	srl r0,8

     EEC0 0003

11355               	; 	' 118, NOT 120, AND THE TWO HAVE TO BE WORKED OUT THE SAME WAY. A marker
11356               	; 	' is three pixels wide and the car is five, so putting both left edges at
11357               	; 	' the same x leaves their CENTRES two apart -- and an actor standing dead
11358               	; 	' centre in the lift read as standing at its left-hand edge.
11359               	; 	'
11360               	; 	' An actor in the shaft is at store x 120 on screen 3, which is canvas
11361               	; 	' 3*16 + 120/16 = 55, so his three pixels are screen 119-121 and his
11362               	; 	' centre is 120. Five pixels centred on 120 start at 118. The character
11363               	; 	' version had the same two-pixel error (char column 7, bits 248, screen

12037               	;
12038               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
12039               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
12040               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
12041               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
12042               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
12043               	; 	'
12044               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
12045               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
12046               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
12047               	; 	'
12048               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
12049               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
12050               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
12051               	; 	'
12052               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
12053               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12054               	; bonus_count:

     F56E F4C0     
12157               	;
12158               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
12159               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
12160               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
12161               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
12162               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
12163               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
12164               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
12165               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
12166               	; 	'
12167               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
12168               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
12169               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12170               	;
12171               	; do_escape:

     F69E AA16     
12305               	;
12306               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
12307               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
12308               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
12309               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
12310               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
12311               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
12312               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
12313               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
12314               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
12315               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
12316               	; 	' Harry was caught.
12317               	; snd_off:
12318               cvb_SND_OFF
12319               	; 	SOUND 0,0,0
12320 F6A0 04C0  14 	clr r0
12321 F6A2 0202  20 	li r2,>8000
     F6A4 8000     
12322 F6A6 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     F6A8 A980     
12323 F6AA A2BA     	data sn76489_freq
12324 F6AC 04C0  14 	clr r0
12325 F6AE 0202  20 	li r2,>9000
     F6B0 9000     
12326 F6B2 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     F6B4 A980

     F6BC A000

     F6C0 A980

     F6C8 B000

     F6CC A980

12358               	; 	' AND THE EFFECTS THAT HAVE NOT HAPPENED YET. A set sf* flag is a sound
12359               	; 	' waiting for the next pass of the main loop -- and between a capture and
12360               	; 	' the next Krook the main loop does not run, so anything latched during
12361               	; 	' the round (a hit as Harry was caught, the bonus Kop the tally just
12362               	; 	' awarded) survived the silence and fired on the first pass of the NEW
12363               	; 	' round. Silencing the channels does not help: the flag plays a fresh
12364               	; 	' note afterwards, with a fresh decay counter, which is why it came out
12365               	; 	' as one long tone over the start of a level that had earned nothing.
12366               	; 	sfj = 0

12416               	; 	' ======================================================================
12417               	; 	' SOUND
12418               	; 	' ======================================================================
12419               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
12420               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
12421               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
12422               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
12423               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.

12714 F9F6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SHT,r0
     F9F8 21EC     
12715 F9FA 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     F9FC 0100     
12716 F9FE 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
12717 FA00 1402  14 	jhe cv956
12718 FA02 0460  28 	b @cv955
     FA04 FA34     
12719               cv956
12720               	; 		sht = sht - 1
12721 FA06 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SHT,r0
     FA08 21EC     
12722 FA0A 0220  22 	ai r0,65280	; -1
     FA0C FF00

     FA10 21EC

12725 FA12 04C1  14 	clr r1
12726 FA14 9001  18 	cb r1,r0
12727 FA16 1302  14 	jeq cv958
12728 FA18 0460  28 	b @cv957
     FA1A FA34     
12729               cv958

     FA20 A000     
12732 FA22 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     FA24 A980

12734 FA28 04C0  14 	clr r0
12735 FA2A 0202  20 	li r2,>b000
     FA2C B000

     FA30 A980

12738               cv957

12740               cv955

     FA36 2204

     FA3A 0100

     FA42 FA72

12770 FA72 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
12771 FA74 0450  20 	b *r0
12772               	;
12773               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
12774               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
12775               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
12776               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
12777               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12778               	; 	'
12779               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
12780               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
12781               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
12782               	; 	'
12783               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
12784               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
12785               	; 	' letter after it.
12786               	;
12787               	; font_bits:
12788               cvb_FONT_BITS
12789               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space
12790 FA76 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00

12918               	bank 3
12919               	; 	#endif
12920               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
12921               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12922               	; 	'
12923               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
12924               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
12925               	; 	' being small sprites.
12926               	; 	'
12927               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
12928               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
12929               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
12930               	; 	'
12931               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
12932               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
12933               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12934               	;
12935               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
12936               cvb_SPR_KELLY

13768               	;
13769               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
13770               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
13771               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
13772               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
13773               	;
13774               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
13775               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
13776               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
13777               	; 	' unreadable.
13778               	;
13779               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set
13780               cvb_SCAN_COL3
13781               	; 	DATA BYTE $EE,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EC,$EC,$EC,$BC
13782 6C4A EEEE     	byte >ee,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ec,>ec,>ec,>bc
     6C4C EEEE     
     6C4E ECEC     
     6C50 ECBC

13882               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
13883               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
13884               	; 	'
13885               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
13886               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
13887               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
13888               	; 	'
13889               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
13890               	; 	' blit is
13891               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
13892               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
13893               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
13894               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
13895               	; 	'
13896               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
13897               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
13898               	; 	'
13899               	; 	' template ids:
13900               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
13901               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
13902               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
13903               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
13904               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
13905               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
13906               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
13907               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
13908               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
13909               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
13910               	;
13911               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
13912               cvb_STOR_TPL
